
==============================================================================
ANALYSIS 14 - REVIEWER RESPONSES
==============================================================================
  databases   34
  bootstrap   10,000 resamples, seed 20260811
  unit        EPISODE (independently seeded => i.i.d.)
  A revealed-opponent stratification   E score dose-response
  B model heterogeneity                F multiplicity
  C arm-1 CPR denominator              G near-tie / action mass
  D the 3c overshoot                   H 3b round parity

==============================================================================
exp2_llama   model llama   readout logit
==============================================================================
  arms ['1', '3', '3b', '3c', '3d']   opponents ['allc', 'tft']
  NOTE: turns.displayed_opponent_last is absent (pre-exp6 schema).
  Parts D(i), D(ii), D(iii) and E's move-clean slope are skipped;
  which rows were falsified was never recorded for this run.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  A. REVEALED-OPPONENT STRATIFICATION
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Until the agent defects, TFT and ALLC have emitted an identical
    sequence, so no arm contains a bit that distinguishes them. The
    pre-registered sign-flip is untestable on those turns.

    opp   stratum        P(D|3)  P(D|3b)       ATE              95% CI       p   n_ep
    allc  all turns      0.0797   0.0913   -0.0116   [-0.0196,-0.0038]  0.0046   1600
    allc  pre-reveal     0.1026   0.1200   -0.0174   [-0.0283,-0.0064]  0.0016   1600
    allc  revealed       0.1260   0.1546   -0.0286   [-0.0450,-0.0126]  0.0006   1036
    tft   all turns      0.1045   0.1096   -0.0051   [-0.0151,+0.0048]  0.3248   1600
    tft   pre-reveal     0.1086   0.1167   -0.0081   [-0.0197,+0.0029]  0.1452   1600
    tft   revealed       0.1910   0.1956   -0.0047   [-0.0237,+0.0143]  0.6454   1063

    BASE RATE - episodes that EVER reach the revealed state
    opp                arm 3            arm 3b   selection gap
    allc     1,085/1,600 67.8%   1,036/1,600 64.8%   +0.031
    tft      1,098/1,600 68.6%   1,063/1,600 66.4%   +0.022

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   UNDERPOWERED   the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  UNDERPOWERED   untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    UNDERPOWERED   the objection's own test

    VERDICT: inconclusive on the revealed stratum. Only
      67.8% of episodes reach it in the smallest cell, so the
      study cannot resolve opponent-conditional play. That is a
      limitation to state, not a rejection to claim.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.031 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model llama   readout logit

    arm          allc         tft   what a CPR of 1.000 would mean here
    1           0.200       0.200   reconstruction from raw history - the real denominator
    3           1.000       1.000   every probe target printed verbatim; certifies COPYING
    3b          0.200       0.200   no true state in the block; this is a floor
    3c          0.200       0.223   belief in the donor's numbers, not comprehension
    3d          0.000       0.000   

    ARM 1 CPR BY TURN INDEX  (pooled over opponents; arm 3 for scale)
     turn   arm1 CPR       n   arm3 CPR       n
        0      1.000   3,200      1.000   3,200
        4      0.000   3,200      1.000   3,200
        8      0.000   3,200      1.000   3,200
       12      0.000   3,200      1.000   3,200
       16      0.000   3,200      1.000   3,200

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.200. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 20.0% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.500. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  I. WHICH FIELD FAILS - decomposing arm-1 CPR
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model llama   readout logit

    ARM 1 - state must be reconstructed from [HISTORY]
     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played
                     ARITHMETIC                RECALL              COUNTING
        0                 1.000                 1.000                 1.000
        4                 0.000                 0.998                 1.000
        8                 0.000                 0.992                 1.000
       12                 0.000                 0.972                 1.000
       16                 0.000                 0.985                 1.000

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.000    1.000   +1.000   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.987    1.000   +0.013   TRACKED without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        1.000    1.000   +0.000   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: USED-FIELD TRACKED. Without the block the model
    recalls the opponent's last move (0.987) but cannot
    reconstruct its own score (0.000). exp6 showed the
    last move is the ONLY field whose falsification moves
    behaviour. So the block repairs the field the model was
    already failing AND already ignoring, which is why perfect
    reading buys no behavioural change. Arm-1 CPR of ~0.00 is
    then an ARITHMETIC result and must not be reported as
    'the model cannot track the state'.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  F(inputs). CONTRAST FAMILY  (episode-level, 10,000 resamples)
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    'quotable' drops turn 0 only where an arm is byte-identical to
    arm 3 there (3c, 3m). Arm 3s is
    falsified at turn 0 too, so dropping it would discard real
    manipulation - analysis/13 drops it from every contrast.

    contrast                opp      incl t0  quotable               95% CI       p  t0
    ATE_true       3 - 3b   allc     -0.0116   -0.0116   [-0.0198,-0.0038]  0.0016   0 *
    ATE_true       3 - 3b   tft      -0.0051   -0.0051   [-0.0154,+0.0048]  0.3096   0  
    content_donor  3 - 3c   allc     -0.0109   -0.0113   [-0.0182,-0.0046]  0.0006   1 *
    content_donor  3 - 3c   tft      -0.0177   -0.0186   [-0.0280,-0.0091]  0.0001   1 *
    perturbation  3b - 1    allc     -0.2154   -0.2154   [-0.2285,-0.2023]  0.0001   0 *
    perturbation  3b - 1    tft      -0.2128   -0.2128   [-0.2272,-0.1992]  0.0001   0 *

    * = raw bootstrap CI excludes zero, BEFORE any multiplicity
    correction. Part F decides which of these survive.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

    arm  opp           n  mass p05      p50      p95  |gap| p50   <0.10   <0.25
    1    allc     32,000     0.998    0.999    0.999      0.596   0.000   0.000 *
    1    tft      32,000     0.998    0.999    1.000      0.554   0.000   0.000
    3    allc     32,000     1.000    1.000    1.000      0.865   0.000   0.000
    3    tft      32,000     1.000    1.000    1.000      0.865   0.000   0.000
    3b   allc     32,000     0.999    0.999    1.000      0.879   0.000   0.000 *
    3b   tft      32,000     0.999    0.999    1.000      0.864   0.000   0.000
    3c   allc     32,000     1.000    1.000    1.000      0.830   0.000   0.000
    3c   tft      32,000     0.999    1.000    1.000      0.809   0.000   0.000
    3d   allc     32,000     0.998    0.999    0.999      0.703   0.000   0.000
    3d   tft      32,000     0.998    0.999    0.999      0.635   0.000   0.000

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: only 0.0% of decisions in the largest-effect
    cells are fragile (mass < 0.25). The estimates are not
    an artefact of near-ties.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0339    -0.0307     [-0.0361,-0.0251]  0.0001       +0.0600
    3b   tft       -0.0341    -0.0277     [-0.0342,-0.0212]  0.0001       +0.0981
    1    allc      -0.0285    -0.0060     [-0.0146,+0.0028]  0.1772       +0.2483
    1    tft       -0.0312    -0.0073     [-0.0160,+0.0017]  0.1064       +0.3050

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.0222   0.0156      even
        1   0.0600   0.1797       odd
        2   0.0394   0.1512      even
        3   0.0819   0.1703       odd
        4   0.0709   0.2462      even
        5   0.1053   0.2781       odd
        6   0.0791   0.2806      even
        7   0.1134   0.3003       odd
        8   0.0788   0.3187      even
        9   0.1147   0.3350       odd
       10   0.1009   0.3575      even
       11   0.1194   0.3556       odd
       12   0.0897   0.3744      even
       13   0.1534   0.3819       odd
       14   0.1103   0.3997      even
       15   0.1475   0.4094       odd
       16   0.1256   0.4053      even
       17   0.1319   0.4144       odd
       18   0.1175   0.4469      even
       19   0.1472   0.4703       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    Arm 1, which has no parity line, shows no detrended effect -
    so the block is the source.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.0791, arm 1 +0.2766.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

  I. WHICH FIELD FAILS - decomposing arm-1 CPR

    ARM 1 - state must be reconstructed from [HISTORY]
     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played
                     ARITHMETIC                RECALL              COUNTING
        0                 1.000                 1.000                 1.000
        4                 0.000                 0.041                 1.000
        8                 0.000                 0.017                 1.000
       12                 0.000                 0.041                 1.000
       16                 0.000                 0.019                 1.000

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.000      nan     +nan   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.030      nan     +nan   FAILS without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        1.000      nan     +nan   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: BOTH FAIL. Neither the arithmetic field nor the
    one-line-recall field survives without the block (0.030,
    0.000). State tracking fails in the ordinary sense, the
    block repairs something real, and repairing it still does not
    produce opponent-conditional play. This is the STRONGER
    version of the paper's claim - the conjunction is not
    carrying it, and 'comprehension is not the bottleneck' is
    earned rather than inferred.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0516    +0.0003     [-0.0010,+0.0017]  0.6170       +0.1794
    3b   tft       -0.0527    +0.0004     [-0.0011,+0.0019]  0.6030       +0.2004
    1    allc      -0.0033    -0.0007     [-0.0017,+0.0004]  0.2114       +0.0229
    1    tft       -0.0036    +0.0001     [-0.0011,+0.0012]  0.9230       +0.0262

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.0000   0.0003      even
        1   0.0000   0.0003       odd
        2   0.0000   0.0003      even
        3   0.0003   0.0000       odd
        4   0.0009   0.0003      even
        5   0.0019   0.0003       odd
        6   0.0078   0.0006      even
        7   0.0100   0.0022       odd
        8   0.0253   0.0041      even
        9   0.0356   0.0047       odd
       10   0.0400   0.0069      even
       11   0.0484   0.0128       odd
       12   0.0512   0.0122      even
       13   0.0578   0.0109       odd
       14   0.0638   0.0112      even
       15   0.0778   0.0163       odd
       16   0.0828   0.0181      even
       17   0.0938   0.0213       odd
       18   0.1144   0.0244      even
       19   0.5819   0.0441       odd

    VERDICT: no detrended parity effect in any 3b cell. The raw
    even-odd column is the turn trend and nothing else. The
    placebo's one real bit of state does not move play - which is
    what a placebo has to demonstrate rather than assert.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.1899, arm 1 +0.0246.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

    0.000). State tracking fails in the ordinary sense, the
    block repairs something real, and repairing it still does not
    produce opponent-conditional play. This is the STRONGER
    version of the paper's claim - the conjunction is not
    carrying it, and 'comprehension is not the bottleneck' is
    earned rather than inferred.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  A. REVEALED-OPPONENT STRATIFICATION
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Until the agent defects, TFT and ALLC have emitted an identical
    sequence, so no arm contains a bit that distinguishes them. The

    allc  revealed       0.7237   0.7591   -0.0354   [-0.0449,-0.0260]  0.0001   2000
    tft   all turns      0.5987   0.7715   -0.1728   [-0.1818,-0.1640]  0.0001   2000
    tft   pre-reveal     0.7680   0.6738   +0.0942   [+0.0743,+0.1134]  0.0001   2000
    tft   revealed       0.5951   0.8191   -0.2240   [-0.2325,-0.2154]  0.0001   2000

    BASE RATE - episodes that EVER reach the revealed state
    opp                arm 3            arm 3b   selection gap
    allc     2,000/2,000100.0%   2,000/2,000100.0%   +0.000
    tft      2,000/2,000100.0%   2,000/2,000100.0%   +0.000

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   UNDERPOWERED   the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  REJECTED       untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    REJECTED       the objection's own test

    VERDICT: the rejection survives the strongest defence of the
      hypothesis. Restricted to turns where the opponent's type IS
      revealed, the sign-flip still fails. Report the stratified
      test in the paper - it forecloses this objection.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.000 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

    rounds played         COUNTING        1.000    1.000   +0.000   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: USED-FIELD TRACKED. Without the block the model
    recalls the opponent's last move (0.971) but cannot
    reconstruct its own score (0.059). exp6 showed the
    last move is the ONLY field whose falsification moves
    behaviour. So the block repairs the field the model was
    already failing AND already ignoring, which is why perfect
    reading buys no behavioural change. Arm-1 CPR of ~0.00 is
    then an ARITHMETIC result and must not be reported as
    'the model cannot track the state'.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  E. SCORE DOSE-RESPONSE - is '29x' a dose or a field?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3s lies by a FIXED 15 points and arm 3m flips a
    binary field 100% of the time. The '29x' ratio between them is
    therefore a statement about DOSE as much as about FIELD. Arm 3c is
    the only arm whose score error varies, so it is the only arm that
    can put a slope on the score - a slope that predicts what a 15-point
    lie should do, testable against what arm 3s actually did.

    The 3c slope inherits analysis/11's confound: |error| is large
    exactly when the recipient's own score ran unusually, which happens

    * = raw bootstrap CI excludes zero, BEFORE any multiplicity
    correction. Part F decides which of these survive.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0138    -0.0015     [-0.0081,+0.0054]  0.6730       +0.0702
    3b   tft       -0.0126    +0.0005     [-0.0062,+0.0071]  0.8806       +0.1395
    1    allc      -0.0229    -0.0116     [-0.0191,-0.0044]  0.0014       +0.1664
    1    tft       -0.0266    -0.0104     [-0.0174,-0.0033]  0.0036       +0.2010

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.4900   0.4360      even
        1   0.6118   0.5200       odd
        2   0.6653   0.5867      even
        3   0.7262   0.6298       odd
        4   0.7448   0.6947      even
        5   0.7360   0.7752       odd
        6   0.7940   0.8163      even
        7   0.8187   0.8277       odd
        8   0.8247   0.8215      even
        9   0.7873   0.7963       odd
       10   0.7940   0.7568      even
       11   0.8015   0.7748       odd
       12   0.7785   0.7435      even
       13   0.7340   0.7622       odd
       14   0.7800   0.7632      even
       15   0.7798   0.8017       odd
       16   0.7712   0.7740      even
       17   0.7648   0.7502       odd
       18   0.7160   0.7288      even
       19   0.7305   0.7310       odd

    VERDICT: no detrended parity effect in any 3b cell. The raw
    even-odd column is the turn trend and nothing else. The
    placebo's one real bit of state does not move play - which is
    what a placebo has to demonstrate rather than assert.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.1048, arm 1 +0.1837.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  A. REVEALED-OPPONENT STRATIFICATION
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Until the agent defects, TFT and ALLC have emitted an identical
    sequence, so no arm contains a bit that distinguishes them. The
    pre-registered sign-flip is untestable on those turns.

    opp   stratum        P(D|3)  P(D|3b)       ATE              95% CI       p   n_ep
    allc  all turns      0.0850   0.0985   -0.0135   [-0.0210,-0.0060]  0.0002   2000
    allc  pre-reveal     0.1092   0.1338   -0.0246   [-0.0355,-0.0141]  0.0001   2000
    allc  revealed       0.1418   0.1492   -0.0074   [-0.0227,+0.0075]  0.3336   1358
    tft   all turns      0.0998   0.1205   -0.0207   [-0.0300,-0.0117]  0.0001   2000

    BASE RATE - episodes that EVER reach the revealed state
    opp                arm 3            arm 3b   selection gap
    allc     1,358/2,000 67.9%   1,381/2,000 69.0%   -0.011
    tft      1,353/2,000 67.7%   1,368/2,000 68.4%   -0.008

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   REJECTED       the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  REJECTED       untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    UNDERPOWERED   the objection's own test

    VERDICT: inconclusive on the revealed stratum. Only
      67.7% of episodes reach it in the smallest cell, so the
      study cannot resolve opponent-conditional play. That is a
      limitation to state, not a rejection to claim.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.011 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model llama   readout logit

    arm          allc         tft   what a CPR of 1.000 would mean here
    1           0.200       0.200   reconstruction from raw history - the real denominator
    3           1.000       1.000   every probe target printed verbatim; certifies COPYING
    3b          0.200       0.200   no true state in the block; this is a floor
    3c          0.200       0.220   belief in the donor's numbers, not comprehension
    3d          0.000       0.000   

    ARM 1 CPR BY TURN INDEX  (pooled over opponents; arm 3 for scale)
     turn   arm1 CPR       n   arm3 CPR       n
        0      1.000   4,000      1.000   4,000

        8      0.000   4,000      1.000   4,000
       12      0.000   4,000      1.000   4,000
       16      0.000   4,000      1.000   4,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.200. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 20.0% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.500. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  I. WHICH FIELD FAILS - decomposing arm-1 CPR
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model llama   readout logit

    ARM 1 - state must be reconstructed from [HISTORY]
     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played
                     ARITHMETIC                RECALL              COUNTING
        0                 1.000                 1.000                 1.000
        4                 0.000                 0.999                 1.000
        8                 0.000                 0.997                 1.000
       12                 0.000                 0.966                 1.000
       16                 0.000                 0.985                 1.000

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.000    1.000   +1.000   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.986    1.000   +0.014   TRACKED without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        1.000    1.000   +0.000   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: USED-FIELD TRACKED. Without the block the model
    recalls the opponent's last move (0.986) but cannot
    reconstruct its own score (0.000). exp6 showed the
    last move is the ONLY field whose falsification moves
    behaviour. So the block repairs the field the model was
    already failing AND already ignoring, which is why perfect
    reading buys no behavioural change. Arm-1 CPR of ~0.00 is
    then an ARITHMETIC result and must not be reported as
    'the model cannot track the state'.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  E. SCORE DOSE-RESPONSE - is '29x' a dose or a field?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3s lies by a FIXED 15 points and arm 3m flips a
    binary field 100% of the time. The '29x' ratio between them is
    therefore a statement about DOSE as much as about FIELD. Arm 3c is
    the only arm whose score error varies, so it is the only arm that
    can put a slope on the score - a slope that predicts what a 15-point
    lie should do, testable against what arm 3s actually did.

    The 3c slope inherits analysis/11's confound: |error| is large

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    CAUTION: arm 1 also shows a detrended parity effect in
    1|allc, 1|tft, and arm 1 has no parity line. Something
    other than the block produces an alternation here; do not
    attribute 3b's effect to the block until that is explained.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.0821, arm 1 +0.2675.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

       12                 0.000                 0.038                 1.000
       16                 0.000                 0.020                 1.000

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.000    1.000   +1.000   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.030    0.000   -0.030   FAILS without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        1.000    1.000   +0.000   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: BOTH FAIL. Neither the arithmetic field nor the
    one-line-recall field survives without the block (0.030,
    0.000). State tracking fails in the ordinary sense, the
    block repairs something real, and repairing it still does not
    produce opponent-conditional play. This is the STRONGER
    version of the paper's claim - the conjunction is not
    carrying it, and 'comprehension is not the bottleneck' is
    earned rather than inferred.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  D. THE 3c OVERSHOOT - three explanations, all testable
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    true-score reconstruction check: 0 at turn 0 in every episode - OK

    (i) ARE 3c's FALSIFIED ROWS LANDING ON HIGH-LEVERAGE TURNS?

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  E. SCORE DOSE-RESPONSE - is '29x' a dose or a field?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3s lies by a FIXED 15 points and arm 3m flips a
    binary field 100% of the time. The '29x' ratio between them is
    therefore a statement about DOSE as much as about FIELD. Arm 3c is
    the only arm whose score error varies, so it is the only arm that
    can put a slope on the score - a slope that predicts what a 15-point
    lie should do, testable against what arm 3s actually did.

    vs allc  (38,000 non-degenerate rows)
         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
             1-4   21,200      0.4230   [+0.4105,+0.4327]
             5-9    8,765      0.2588   [+0.2602,+0.2861]
           10-14    5,911      0.2341   [+0.2166,+0.2443]
           15-19    1,405      0.2477   [+0.2033,+0.2507]
             20+      719      0.2921   [+0.2259,+0.2961]

      slope, all 3c rows        -0.01641 per point   [-0.01758,-0.01529]
      slope, move NOT falsified  SKIPPED - displayed_opponent_last absent, so the pooled slope is
                                 contaminated by the move field.
      implied 15-point effect  -0.2461   [-0.2637,-0.2294]
      arm 3s absent - cannot compare to a measured 15-point effect.

         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
               0      686      0.7522   [+0.7008,+0.7697]

      slope, move NOT falsified  SKIPPED - displayed_opponent_last absent, so the pooled slope is
                                 contaminated by the move field.
      implied 15-point effect  -0.1978   [-0.2112,-0.1841]
      arm 3s absent - cannot compare to a measured 15-point effect.

    The 3c slope inherits analysis/11's confound: |error| is large
    exactly when the recipient's own score ran unusually, which happens
    when it was already defecting. Read the slope as an upper bound.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

    arm  opp           n  mass p05      p50      p95  |gap| p50   <0.10   <0.25
    1    allc     40,000     0.999    0.999    1.000      0.829   0.000   0.000
    1    tft      40,000     0.998    0.999    1.000      0.635   0.000   0.000 *
    3    allc     40,000     0.999    1.000    1.000      0.554   0.000   0.000
    3    tft      40,000     0.999    1.000    1.000      0.786   0.000   0.000
    3b   allc     40,000     0.999    0.999    1.000      0.635   0.000   0.000
    3b   tft      40,000     0.999    0.999    1.000      0.786   0.000   0.000 *
    3c   allc     40,000     0.999    1.000    1.000      0.554   0.000   0.000

    3d   tft      40,000     0.998    0.999    0.999      0.554   0.000   0.000

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: only 0.0% of decisions in the largest-effect
    cells are fragile (mass < 0.25). The estimates are not
    an artefact of near-ties.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      +0.1181    +0.0819     [+0.0748,+0.0891]  0.0001       -0.5460
    3b   tft       +0.0730    +0.0616     [+0.0549,+0.0688]  0.0001       -0.1203
    1    allc      +0.0478    +0.0052     [-0.0014,+0.0115]  0.1184       -0.4505
    1    tft       +0.0515    +0.0236     [+0.0163,+0.0311]  0.0001       -0.2573

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.9798   0.9842      even
        1   0.7550   0.6042       odd
        2   0.7867   0.5557      even
        3   0.6068   0.4480       odd
        4   0.6220   0.3957      even
        5   0.5072   0.3395       odd
        6   0.5625   0.3137      even
        7   0.4810   0.3265       odd
        8   0.5820   0.2828      even
        9   0.4660   0.2823       odd
       10   0.5025   0.2802      even
       11   0.4412   0.2680       odd
       12   0.4675   0.2635      even
       13   0.4128   0.2735       odd
       14   0.4740   0.2500      even
       15   0.4100   0.2542       odd
       16   0.4330   0.2435      even
       17   0.4108   0.2625       odd
       18   0.4333   0.2220      even
       19   0.3975   0.2362       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    CAUTION: arm 1 also shows a detrended parity effect in
    1|tft, and arm 1 has no parity line. Something
    other than the block produces an alternation here; do not
    attribute 3b's effect to the block until that is explained.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b -0.3332, arm 1 -0.3539.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  A. REVEALED-OPPONENT STRATIFICATION
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Until the agent defects, TFT and ALLC have emitted an identical
    sequence, so no arm contains a bit that distinguishes them. The
    pre-registered sign-flip is untestable on those turns.

    opp   stratum        P(D|3)  P(D|3b)       ATE              95% CI       p   n_ep
    allc  all turns      0.8464   0.8386   +0.0078   [+0.0035,+0.0120]  0.0002   2000
    allc  pre-reveal     0.4243   0.2927   +0.1316   [+0.1256,+0.1377]  0.0001   2000
    allc  revealed       0.9114   0.9825   -0.0710   [-0.0743,-0.0678]  0.0001   2000
    tft   all turns      0.6055   0.8322   -0.2266   [-0.2310,-0.2222]  0.0001   2000
    tft   pre-reveal     0.4242   0.2981   +0.1261   [+0.1199,+0.1321]  0.0001   2000
    tft   revealed       0.6371   0.9698   -0.3327   [-0.3371,-0.3284]  0.0001   2000

    BASE RATE - episodes that EVER reach the revealed state
    opp                arm 3            arm 3b   selection gap
    allc     2,000/2,000100.0%   2,000/2,000100.0%   +0.000
    tft      2,000/2,000100.0%   2,000/2,000100.0%   +0.000

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   SUPPORTED      the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  REJECTED       untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    REJECTED       the objection's own test

    VERDICT: the rejection survives the strongest defence of the
      hypothesis. Restricted to turns where the opponent's type IS
      revealed, the sign-flip still fails. Report the stratified
      test in the paper - it forecloses this objection.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.000 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model mistral   readout logit

    arm          allc         tft   what a CPR of 1.000 would mean here
    1           0.206       0.239   reconstruction from raw history - the real denominator
    3           1.000       0.996   every probe target printed verbatim; certifies COPYING
    3b          0.000       0.001   no true state in the block; this is a floor
    3c          0.200       0.232   belief in the donor's numbers, not comprehension
    3d          0.200       0.373   

    ARM 1 CPR BY TURN INDEX  (pooled over opponents; arm 3 for scale)
     turn   arm1 CPR       n   arm3 CPR       n
        0      1.000   4,000      1.000   4,000
        4      0.043   4,000      1.000   4,000
        8      0.002   4,000      1.000   4,000
       12      0.049   4,000      0.994   4,000
       16      0.021   4,000      0.998   4,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.223. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 22.3% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.500. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  I. WHICH FIELD FAILS - decomposing arm-1 CPR
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model mistral   readout logit

    ARM 1 - state must be reconstructed from [HISTORY]
     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played
                     ARITHMETIC                RECALL              COUNTING
        0                 1.000                 1.000                 1.000
        4                 0.043                 0.711                 1.000
        8                 0.002                 0.887                 1.000
       12                 0.049                 0.946                 1.000
       16                 0.023                 0.969                 0.885

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.029    1.000   +0.971   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.878    0.998   +0.120   TRACKED without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        0.971    1.000   +0.029   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: USED-FIELD TRACKED. Without the block the model
    recalls the opponent's last move (0.878) but cannot
    reconstruct its own score (0.029). exp6 showed the
    last move is the ONLY field whose falsification moves
    behaviour. So the block repairs the field the model was
    already failing AND already ignoring, which is why perfect
    reading buys no behavioural change. Arm-1 CPR of ~0.00 is
    then an ARITHMETIC result and must not be reported as
    'the model cannot track the state'.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.5787, arm 1 +0.5578.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  A. REVEALED-OPPONENT STRATIFICATION
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Until the agent defects, TFT and ALLC have emitted an identical
    sequence, so no arm contains a bit that distinguishes them. The
    pre-registered sign-flip is untestable on those turns.

    opp   stratum        P(D|3)  P(D|3b)       ATE              95% CI       p   n_ep
    allc  all turns      0.0001   0.0004   -0.0003   [-0.0005,-0.0001]  0.0070   2000
    allc  pre-reveal     0.0004   0.0009   -0.0005   [-0.0013,+0.0002]  0.1766   2000
    allc  revealed       0.0000   0.0000   +0.0000   [+0.0000,+0.0000]  1.0000      3
    tft   all turns      0.0002   0.0001   +0.0001   [-0.0000,+0.0003]  0.2076   2000
    tft   pre-reveal     0.0006   0.0001   +0.0006   [+0.0000,+0.0012]  0.0248   2000
    tft   revealed       0.0000   0.0000   +0.0000   [+0.0000,+0.0000]  1.0000      2

    BASE RATE - episodes that EVER reach the revealed state
    opp                arm 3            arm 3b   selection gap
    allc         3/2,000  0.1%      14/2,000  0.7%   -0.005
    tft          6/2,000  0.3%       2/2,000  0.1%   +0.002

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   UNDERPOWERED   the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  UNDERPOWERED   untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    UNDERPOWERED   the objection's own test

    VERDICT: inconclusive on the revealed stratum. Only
      0.1% of episodes reach it in the smallest cell, so the
      study cannot resolve opponent-conditional play. That is a
      limitation to state, not a rejection to claim.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.005 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model mistral   readout logit

    arm          allc         tft   what a CPR of 1.000 would mean here
    1           0.400       0.400   reconstruction from raw history - the real denominator
    3           1.000       1.000   every probe target printed verbatim; certifies COPYING
    3b          0.400       0.400   no true state in the block; this is a floor
    3c          0.200       0.200   belief in the donor's numbers, not comprehension
    3d          0.400       0.400

     turn   arm1 CPR       n   arm3 CPR       n

       12      0.000   4,000      1.000   4,000
       16      0.000   4,000      1.000   4,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.400. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 40.0% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -1.000. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  E. SCORE DOSE-RESPONSE - is '29x' a dose or a field?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3s lies by a FIXED 15 points and arm 3m flips a
    binary field 100% of the time. The '29x' ratio between them is
    therefore a statement about DOSE as much as about FIELD. Arm 3c is
    the only arm whose score error varies, so it is the only arm that
    can put a slope on the score - a slope that predicts what a 15-point
    lie should do, testable against what arm 3s actually did.

    vs allc  (34,000 non-degenerate rows)
         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
             1-4   34,000      0.0000   [+0.0000,+0.0001]

      slope, all 3c rows        +nan per point   [+nan,+nan]
      slope, move NOT falsified  SKIPPED - displayed_opponent_last absent, so the pooled slope is
                                 contaminated by the move field.
      implied 15-point effect  +nan   [+nan,+nan]
      arm 3s absent - cannot compare to a measured 15-point effect.

    vs tft  (34,000 non-degenerate rows)
         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
             1-4   34,000      0.0001   [+0.0000,+0.0002]

      slope, all 3c rows        -0.00013 per point   [-0.00025,-0.00003]
      slope, move NOT falsified  SKIPPED - displayed_opponent_last absent, so the pooled slope is
                                 contaminated by the move field.
      implied 15-point effect  -0.0019   [-0.0038,-0.0005]
      arm 3s absent - cannot compare to a measured 15-point effect.

    The 3c slope inherits analysis/11's confound: |error| is large
    exactly when the recipient's own score ran unusually, which happens
    when it was already defecting. Read the slope as an upper bound.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  F(inputs). CONTRAST FAMILY  (episode-level, 10,000 resamples)
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    'quotable' drops turn 0 only where an arm is byte-identical to
    arm 3 there (3c, 3m). Arm 3s is
    falsified at turn 0 too, so dropping it would discard real
    manipulation - analysis/13 drops it from every contrast.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

    arm  opp           n  mass p05      p50      p95  |gap| p50   <0.10   <0.25
    1    allc     40,000     0.886    0.926    0.965      0.926   0.000   0.000
    1    tft      40,000     0.886    0.926    0.965      0.926   0.000   0.000
    3    allc     40,000     0.795    0.902    0.971      0.902   0.000   0.000 *

    3b   allc     40,000     0.823    0.938    0.977      0.938   0.000   0.000 *
    3b   tft      40,000     0.816    0.945    0.977      0.945   0.000   0.000
    3c   allc     40,000     0.794    0.920    0.973      0.920   0.000   0.000
    3c   tft      40,000     0.768    0.897    0.970      0.897   0.000   0.000
    3d   allc     40,000     0.770    0.974    0.992      0.974   0.000   0.000
    3d   tft      40,000     0.769    0.974    0.992      0.974   0.000   0.000

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: only 0.0% of decisions in the largest-effect
    cells are fragile (mass < 0.25). The estimates are not
    an artefact of near-ties.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0007    -0.0008     [-0.0012,-0.0004]  0.0001       -0.0001
    3b   tft       -0.0001    -0.0001     [-0.0003,-0.0000]  0.2784       +0.0000
    1    allc      +0.0001    +0.0001     [-0.0002,+0.0003]  0.8414       +0.0000
    1    tft       -0.0001    -0.0001     [-0.0003,-0.0000]  0.2726       +0.0000

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.0000   0.0000      even
        1   0.0003   0.0000       odd
        2   0.0000   0.0000      even
        3   0.0000   0.0000       odd
        4   0.0000   0.0000      even
        5   0.0003   0.0000       odd
        6   0.0000   0.0000      even
        7   0.0008   0.0003       odd
        8   0.0000   0.0000      even
        9   0.0003   0.0003       odd
       10   0.0000   0.0005      even
       11   0.0015   0.0003       odd
       12   0.0000   0.0000      even
       13   0.0010   0.0003       odd
       14   0.0000   0.0003      even
       15   0.0000   0.0000       odd
       16   0.0000   0.0000      even
       17   0.0000   0.0000       odd
       18   0.0000   0.0000      even
       19   0.0000   0.0000       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    Arm 1, which has no parity line, shows no detrended effect -
    so the block is the source.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b -0.0001, arm 1 +0.0000.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

    one-line-recall field survives without the block (0.001,
    0.000). State tracking fails in the ordinary sense, the
    block repairs something real, and repairing it still does not
    produce opponent-conditional play. This is the STRONGER
    version of the paper's claim - the conjunction is not
    carrying it, and 'comprehension is not the bottleneck' is
    earned rather than inferred.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

    3d   allc     40,000     0.741    0.833    0.903      0.375   0.000   0.000
    3d   tft      40,000     0.770    0.959    0.988      0.959   0.000   0.000

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: only 0.0% of decisions in the largest-effect
    cells are fragile (mass < 0.25). The estimates are not
    an artefact of near-ties.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      +0.0842    +0.0726     [+0.0644,+0.0809]  0.0001       -0.1897
    3b   tft       +0.0259    +0.0288     [+0.0267,+0.0310]  0.0001       +0.0541
    1    allc      +0.0365    +0.0146     [+0.0053,+0.0238]  0.0018       -0.1798
    1    tft       +0.0115    +0.0127     [+0.0108,+0.0146]  0.0001       +0.0284

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   1.0000   1.0000      even
        1   0.7385   0.8832       odd
        2   0.9835   0.9140      even
        3   0.9748   0.8030       odd
        4   0.9692   0.8247      even
        5   0.8485   0.8263       odd
        6   0.8652   0.8868      even
        7   0.8785   0.8215       odd
        8   0.9447   0.8185      even
        9   0.8982   0.8828       odd
       10   0.8820   0.8087      even
       11   0.8235   0.8187       odd
       12   0.8570   0.8315      even
       13   0.8315   0.8407       odd
       14   0.8452   0.8263      even
       15   0.8230   0.8140       odd
       16   0.8688   0.7995      even
       17   0.8892   0.8103       odd
       18   0.8932   0.8265      even
       19   0.8528   0.7963       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    CAUTION: arm 1 also shows a detrended parity effect in
    1|allc, 1|tft, and arm 1 has no parity line. Something
    other than the block produces an alternation here; do not
    attribute 3b's effect to the block until that is explained.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b -0.0678, arm 1 -0.0757.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  A. REVEALED-OPPONENT STRATIFICATION
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Until the agent defects, TFT and ALLC have emitted an identical
    sequence, so no arm contains a bit that distinguishes them. The
    pre-registered sign-flip is untestable on those turns.

    opp   stratum        P(D|3)  P(D|3b)       ATE              95% CI       p   n_ep
    allc  all turns      0.7710   0.0259   +0.7451   [+0.7388,+0.7511]  0.0001   2000
    allc  pre-reveal     0.4754   0.0138   +0.4615   [+0.4440,+0.4795]  0.0001   2000
    allc  revealed       0.9214   0.8918   +0.0295   [-0.0105,+0.0758]  0.1622    100
    tft   all turns      0.7704   0.0329   +0.7376   [+0.7296,+0.7454]  0.0001   2000
    tft   pre-reveal     0.4705   0.0161   +0.4545   [+0.4370,+0.4725]  0.0001   2000
    tft   revealed       0.9120   0.9839   -0.0719   [-0.0817,-0.0596]  0.0001    107

    BASE RATE - episodes that EVER reach the revealed state
    opp                arm 3            arm 3b   selection gap
    allc     2,000/2,000100.0%     100/2,000  5.0%   +0.950
    tft      2,000/2,000100.0%     107/2,000  5.3%   +0.947

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   REJECTED       the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  REJECTED       untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    UNDERPOWERED   the objection's own test

    VERDICT: inconclusive on the revealed stratum. Only
      100.0% of episodes reach it in the smallest cell, so the
      study cannot resolve opponent-conditional play. That is a
      limitation to state, not a rejection to claim.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.950 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model qwen2.5   readout logit

    arm          allc         tft   what a CPR of 1.000 would mean here
    1           0.396       0.395   reconstruction from raw history - the real denominator
    3           1.000       1.000   every probe target printed verbatim; certifies COPYING
    3b          0.398       0.397   no true state in the block; this is a floor
    3c          0.200       0.209   belief in the donor's numbers, not comprehension
    3d          0.394       0.393   

    ARM 1 CPR BY TURN INDEX  (pooled over opponents; arm 3 for scale)
     turn   arm1 CPR       n   arm3 CPR       n
        0      1.000   4,000      1.000   4,000
        4      0.978   4,000      1.000   4,000
        8      0.000   4,000      1.000   4,000
       12      0.000   4,000      1.000   4,000
       16      0.000   4,000      1.000   4,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.396. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 39.6% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.989. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  E. SCORE DOSE-RESPONSE - is '29x' a dose or a field?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3s lies by a FIXED 15 points and arm 3m flips a
    binary field 100% of the time. The '29x' ratio between them is
    therefore a statement about DOSE as much as about FIELD. Arm 3c is
    the only arm whose score error varies, so it is the only arm that
    can put a slope on the score - a slope that predicts what a 15-point
    lie should do, testable against what arm 3s actually did.

    vs allc  (38,000 non-degenerate rows)
         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
             1-4   24,473      0.8363   [+0.8333,+0.8408]
             5-9   13,162      0.6539   [+0.6072,+0.6228]
           10-14      364      0.9121   [+0.8667,+0.9371]
           15-19        1                below min bin size

      slope, all 3c rows        -0.03652 per point   [-0.03848,-0.03460]
      slope, move NOT falsified  SKIPPED - displayed_opponent_last absent, so the pooled slope is
                                 contaminated by the move field.
      implied 15-point effect  -0.5478   [-0.5772,-0.5189]
      arm 3s absent - cannot compare to a measured 15-point effect.

    vs tft  (38,000 non-degenerate rows)
         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
               0    2,542      0.4622   [+0.4210,+0.4610]
             1-4   15,732      0.7049   [+0.7101,+0.7289]
             5-9    9,898      0.8734   [+0.8576,+0.8744]

             20+      197      0.8020   [+0.7542,+0.8694]

      slope, all 3c rows        +0.01719 per point   [+0.01606,+0.01830]
      slope, move NOT falsified  SKIPPED - displayed_opponent_last absent, so the pooled slope is
                                 contaminated by the move field.
      implied 15-point effect  +0.2578   [+0.2409,+0.2745]
      arm 3s absent - cannot compare to a measured 15-point effect.

    The 3c slope inherits analysis/11's confound: |error| is large
    exactly when the recipient's own score ran unusually, which happens
    when it was already defecting. Read the slope as an upper bound.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?

    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    'quotable' drops turn 0 only where an arm is byte-identical to

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

    VERDICT: only 0.0% of decisions in the largest-effect
    cells are fragile (mass < 0.25). The estimates are not
    an artefact of near-ties.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0333    +0.0036     [+0.0024,+0.0049]  0.0001       +0.1352
    3b   tft       -0.0333    +0.0036     [+0.0021,+0.0053]  0.0001       +0.1495
    1    allc      -0.0037    -0.0006     [-0.0015,+0.0001]  0.1192       +0.0221
    1    tft       -0.0040    +0.0001     [-0.0010,+0.0011]  0.9374       +0.0341

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.0003   0.0000      even
        1   0.0003   0.0005       odd
        2   0.0003   0.0005      even
        3   0.0008   0.0010       odd
        4   0.0018   0.0018      even
        5   0.0030   0.0030       odd
        6   0.0073   0.0037      even
        7   0.0088   0.0050       odd
        8   0.0260   0.0060      even
        9   0.0265   0.0050       odd
       10   0.0343   0.0073      even
       11   0.0372   0.0138       odd
       12   0.0455   0.0135      even
       13   0.0435   0.0140       odd
       14   0.0500   0.0143      even
       15   0.0563   0.0198       odd
       16   0.0683   0.0232      even
       17   0.0745   0.0245       odd
       18   0.1003   0.0272      even
       19   0.4158   0.0495       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    Arm 1, which has no parity line, shows no detrended effect -
    so the block is the source.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.1423, arm 1 +0.0281.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.000 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model qwen2.5   readout logit

    arm          allc         tft   what a CPR of 1.000 would mean here
    1           0.200       0.200   reconstruction from raw history - the real denominator
    3           0.200       0.200   every probe target printed verbatim; certifies COPYING
    3b          0.200       0.200   no true state in the block; this is a floor
    3c          0.200       0.201   belief in the donor's numbers, not comprehension
    3d          0.200       0.200   

    ARM 1 CPR BY TURN INDEX  (pooled over opponents; arm 3 for scale)
     turn   arm1 CPR       n   arm3 CPR       n
        0      1.000   4,000      1.000   4,000
        4      0.000   4,000      0.000   4,000
        8      0.000   4,000      0.000   4,000
       12      0.000   4,000      0.000   4,000
       16      0.000   4,000      0.000   4,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.200. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 20.0% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.500. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  I. WHICH FIELD FAILS - decomposing arm-1 CPR
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model qwen2.5   readout logit

    ARM 1 - state must be reconstructed from [HISTORY]
     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played
                     ARITHMETIC                RECALL              COUNTING
        0                 1.000                 1.000                 1.000
        4                 0.000                 0.062                 1.000
        8                 0.000                 0.001                 1.000
       12                 0.000                 0.017                 1.000
       16                 0.000                 0.031                 1.000

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.000    1.000   +1.000   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.028    0.000   -0.028   FAILS without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        1.000    1.000   +0.000   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: BOTH FAIL. Neither the arithmetic field nor the
    one-line-recall field survives without the block (0.028,
    0.000). State tracking fails in the ordinary sense, the
    block repairs something real, and repairing it still does not
    produce opponent-conditional play. This is the STRONGER
    version of the paper's claim - the conjunction is not
    carrying it, and 'comprehension is not the bottleneck' is
    earned rather than inferred.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.
