    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.000 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model llama   readout logit

    arm          allc         tft   what a CPR of 1.000 would mean here
    1           0.244       0.246   reconstruction from raw history - the real denominator
    3           1.000       0.999   every probe target printed verbatim; certifies COPYING
    3b          0.200       0.200   no true state in the block; this is a floor

    ARM 1 CPR BY TURN INDEX  (pooled over opponents; arm 3 for scale)
     turn   arm1 CPR       n   arm3 CPR       n
        0      1.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
        4      0.224   2,000      1.000   2,000
        8      0.000   2,000      0.999   2,000
       12      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
       16      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.245. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 24.5% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.612. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  I. WHICH FIELD FAILS - decomposing arm-1 CPR
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model llama   readout logit

    ARM 1 - state must be reconstructed from [HISTORY]
     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played
                     ARITHMETIC                RECALL              COUNTING
        0                 1.000                 1.000                 1.000
        4                 0.224                 0.988                 1.000
        8                 0.000                 0.987                 1.000
       12                 0.000                 0.959                 1.000
       16                 0.000                 0.964                 1.000

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.056    1.000   +0.944   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.974    1.000   +0.026   TRACKED without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        1.000    1.000   +0.000   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: USED-FIELD TRACKED. Without the block the model
    recalls the opponent's last move (0.974) but cannot
    reconstruct its own score (0.056). exp6 showed the
    last move is the ONLY field whose falsification moves
    behaviour. So the block repairs the field the model was
    already failing AND already ignoring, which is why perfect
    reading buys no behavioural change. Arm-1 CPR of ~0.00 is
    then an ARITHMETIC result and must not be reported as
    'the model cannot track the state'.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    SKIPPED - arm 3c absent from this database.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  E. SCORE DOSE-RESPONSE - is '29x' a dose or a field?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    SKIPPED - needs arm 3c and turns.donor_agent_score.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  F(inputs). CONTRAST FAMILY  (episode-level, 10,000 resamples)
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    'quotable' drops turn 0 only where an arm is byte-identical to

    perturbation  3b - 1    tft      +0.0430   +0.0430   [+0.0281,+0.0579]  0.0001   0 *

    * = raw bootstrap CI excludes zero, BEFORE any multiplicity
    correction. Part F decides which of these survive.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

    arm  opp           n  mass p05      p50      p95  |gap| p50   <0.10   <0.25
    1    allc     20,000     0.954    0.977    0.993      0.507   0.000   0.000
    1    tft      20,000     0.897    0.964    0.992      0.587   0.000   0.000
    3    allc     20,000     0.989    0.996    0.999      0.460   0.000   0.000
    3    tft      20,000     0.980    0.993    0.997      0.243   0.000   0.000 *
    3b   allc     20,000     0.842    0.944    0.984      0.535   0.000   0.000
    3b   tft      20,000     0.713    0.914    0.979      0.589   0.000   0.000 *

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: only 0.0% of decisions in the largest-effect
    cells are fragile (mass < 0.25). The estimates are not
    an artefact of near-ties.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0104    +0.0007     [-0.0091,+0.0106]  0.8938       +0.0360
    3b   tft       -0.0017    +0.0113     [+0.0018,+0.0210]  0.0218       +0.1552
    1    allc      -0.0174    -0.0097     [-0.0201,+0.0006]  0.0648       +0.1246
    1    tft       -0.0366    -0.0182     [-0.0284,-0.0077]  0.0018       +0.2044

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.5045   0.4340      even
        1   0.6095   0.5145       odd
        2   0.6865   0.5995      even
        3   0.7405   0.6445       odd
        4   0.7510   0.7020      even
        5   0.7440   0.7630       odd
        6   0.8010   0.7985      even
        7   0.8000   0.8240       odd
        8   0.8260   0.8060      even
        9   0.7675   0.7960       odd
       10   0.7770   0.7385      even
       11   0.7950   0.7690       odd
       12   0.7830   0.7375      even
       13   0.7360   0.7355       odd
       14   0.7790   0.7515      even
       15   0.7830   0.8020       odd
       16   0.7800   0.7545      even
       17   0.7645   0.7365       odd
       18   0.7170   0.7085      even
       19   0.7255   0.7155       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    CAUTION: arm 1 also shows a detrended parity effect in
    1|tft, and arm 1 has no parity line. Something
    other than the block produces an alternation here; do not
    attribute 3b's effect to the block until that is explained.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.0956, arm 1 +0.1645.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

    recalls the opponent's last move (0.985) but cannot
    reconstruct its own score (0.000). exp6 showed the
    last move is the ONLY field whose falsification moves
    behaviour. So the block repairs the field the model was
    already failing AND already ignoring, which is why perfect
    reading buys no behavioural change. Arm-1 CPR of ~0.00 is
    then an ARITHMETIC result and must not be reported as
    'the model cannot track the state'.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  D. THE 3c OVERSHOOT - three explanations, all testable
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    SKIPPED - arm 3c absent from this database.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  E. SCORE DOSE-RESPONSE - is '29x' a dose or a field?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    SKIPPED - needs arm 3c and turns.donor_agent_score.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  F(inputs). CONTRAST FAMILY  (episode-level, 10,000 resamples)
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    'quotable' drops turn 0 only where an arm is byte-identical to
    arm 3 there (3c, 3m). Arm 3s is
    falsified at turn 0 too, so dropping it would discard real
    manipulation - analysis/13 drops it from every contrast.

    contrast                opp      incl t0  quotable               95% CI       p  t0
    ATE_true       3 - 3b   allc     -0.0145   -0.0145   [-0.0252,-0.0041]  0.0058   0 *
    ATE_true       3 - 3b   tft      -0.0228   -0.0228   [-0.0365,-0.0091]  0.0024   0 *
    perturbation  3b - 1    allc     -0.2218   -0.2218   [-0.2391,-0.2046]  0.0001   0 *
    perturbation  3b - 1    tft      -0.2069   -0.2069   [-0.2254,-0.1884]  0.0001   0 *

    * = raw bootstrap CI excludes zero, BEFORE any multiplicity
    correction. Part F decides which of these survive.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

    arm  opp           n  mass p05      p50      p95  |gap| p50   <0.10   <0.25
    1    allc     20,000     0.998    0.999    0.999      0.595   0.000   0.000 *
    1    tft      20,000     0.998    0.999    0.999      0.554   0.000   0.000
    3    allc     20,000     1.000    1.000    1.000      0.865   0.000   0.000
    3    tft      20,000     1.000    1.000    1.000      0.865   0.000   0.000
    3b   allc     20,000     0.999    0.999    1.000      0.864   0.000   0.000 *
    3b   tft      20,000     0.999    0.999    1.000      0.848   0.000   0.000

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: only 0.0% of decisions in the largest-effect
    cells are fragile (mass < 0.25). The estimates are not
    an artefact of near-ties.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0322    -0.0278     [-0.0352,-0.0204]  0.0001       +0.0742
    3b   tft       -0.0375    -0.0285     [-0.0372,-0.0198]  0.0001       +0.1250
    1    allc      -0.0347    -0.0140     [-0.0246,-0.0035]  0.0090       +0.2542
    1    tft       -0.0463    -0.0208     [-0.0319,-0.0097]  0.0006       +0.2926

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.0275   0.0125      even
        1   0.0715   0.2055       odd
        2   0.0465   0.1745      even
        3   0.0895   0.2050       odd
        4   0.0795   0.2430      even
        5   0.1210   0.2960       odd
        6   0.0925   0.2970      even
        7   0.1215   0.3335       odd
        8   0.0845   0.3250      even
        9   0.1335   0.3525       odd
       10   0.1055   0.3575      even
       11   0.1325   0.3895       odd
       12   0.0975   0.3825      even
       13   0.1480   0.3985       odd
       14   0.1510   0.4220      even
       15   0.1740   0.4330       odd
       16   0.1535   0.4075      even
       17   0.1585   0.3970       odd
       18   0.1450   0.4770      even
       19   0.1815   0.4930       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    CAUTION: arm 1 also shows a detrended parity effect in
    1|allc, 1|tft, and arm 1 has no parity line. Something
    other than the block produces an alternation here; do not
    attribute 3b's effect to the block until that is explained.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.0996, arm 1 +0.2734.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

  A. REVEALED-OPPONENT STRATIFICATION
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Until the agent defects, TFT and ALLC have emitted an identical
    sequence, so no arm contains a bit that distinguishes them. The
    pre-registered sign-flip is untestable on those turns.

    opp   stratum        P(D|3)  P(D|3b)       ATE              95% CI       p   n_ep
    allc  all turns      0.5546   0.5787   -0.0242   [-0.0405,-0.0079]  0.0040   1000
    allc  pre-reveal     0.5665   0.5348   +0.0317   [+0.0023,+0.0618]  0.0330   1000
    allc  revealed       0.5842   0.6239   -0.0397   [-0.0562,-0.0231]  0.0001    998
    tft   all turns      0.6120   0.6661   -0.0540   [-0.0714,-0.0372]  0.0001   1000
    tft   pre-reveal     0.5654   0.5225   +0.0429   [+0.0134,+0.0728]  0.0044   1000
    tft   revealed       0.6545   0.7313   -0.0769   [-0.0930,-0.0612]  0.0001    999

    BASE RATE - episodes that EVER reach the revealed state
    opp                arm 3            arm 3b   selection gap
    allc     1,000/1,000100.0%     998/1,000 99.8%   +0.002
    tft      1,000/1,000100.0%     999/1,000 99.9%   +0.001

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   REJECTED       the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  REJECTED       untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    REJECTED       the objection's own test

    VERDICT: the rejection survives the strongest defence of the
      hypothesis. Restricted to turns where the opponent's type IS
      revealed, the sign-flip still fails. Report the stratified
      test in the paper - it forecloses this objection.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.002 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

    1    allc     20,000     0.009    0.132    0.424      0.128   0.436   0.687
    1    tft      20,000     0.008    0.086    0.352      0.076   0.533   0.778
    3    allc     20,000     0.005    0.016    0.049      0.014   1.000   1.000
    3    tft      20,000     0.004    0.009    0.024      0.006   1.000   1.000 *
    3b   allc     20,000     0.003    0.030    0.161      0.029   0.831   1.000
    3b   tft      20,000     0.003    0.015    0.080      0.015   0.970   1.000 *

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: 100.0% of decisions in the largest-effect cells sit
    below action_mass_total 0.25. That effect is partly a
    statement about a renormalisation of a small number. Report
    the fragile share beside the estimate, and check the effect
    survives restricting to solid decisions before quoting it.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0132    +0.0409     [+0.0369,+0.0450]  0.0001       +0.5704
    3b   tft       -0.0121    +0.0421     [+0.0378,+0.0463]  0.0001       +0.5798
    1    allc      -0.0866    -0.0478     [-0.0548,-0.0408]  0.0001       +0.5540
    1    tft       -0.0787    -0.0390     [-0.0467,-0.0312]  0.0001       +0.5672

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.0000   0.1240      even
        1   0.0905   0.6505       odd
        2   0.6350   0.3160      even
        3   0.5920   0.4770       odd
        4   0.8060   0.5820      even
        5   0.8695   0.6890       odd
        6   0.9105   0.8010      even
        7   0.9475   0.8015       odd
        8   0.9930   0.8975      even
        9   0.9750   0.9160       odd
       10   0.9875   0.9420      even
       11   0.9835   0.9625       odd
       12   0.9990   0.9765      even
       13   0.9995   0.9655       odd
       14   1.0000   0.9880      even
       15   0.9995   0.9885       odd
       16   0.9995   0.9900      even
       17   1.0000   0.9905       odd
       18   1.0000   0.9905      even
       19   1.0000   0.9930       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    CAUTION: arm 1 also shows a detrended parity effect in
    1|allc, 1|tft, and arm 1 has no parity line. Something
    other than the block produces an alternation here; do not
    attribute 3b's effect to the block until that is explained.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.5751, arm 1 +0.5606.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

      revealed, the sign-flip still fails. Report the stratified
      test in the paper - it forecloses this objection.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.000 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

       16      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.099. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 9.9% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.247. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  I. WHICH FIELD FAILS - decomposing arm-1 CPR
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model mistral   readout scratchpad

    ARM 1 - state must be reconstructed from [HISTORY]
     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played
                     ARITHMETIC                RECALL              COUNTING
        0                 1.000                 1.000                 0.493
        4                 0.000                 0.995                 1.000
        8                 0.000                 0.976                 1.000
       12                 0.000                 0.922                 1.000
       16                 0.000                 0.900                 0.651

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.000    1.000   +1.000   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.948    1.000   +0.052   TRACKED without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        0.913    1.000   +0.087   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: USED-FIELD TRACKED. Without the block the model
    recalls the opponent's last move (0.948) but cannot
    reconstruct its own score (0.000). exp6 showed the
    last move is the ONLY field whose falsification moves
    behaviour. So the block repairs the field the model was
    already failing AND already ignoring, which is why perfect
    reading buys no behavioural change. Arm-1 CPR of ~0.00 is
    then an ARITHMETIC result and must not be reported as
    'the model cannot track the state'.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

       15   0.0000   0.0005       odd
       16   0.0000   0.0000      even
       17   0.0000   0.0000       odd
       18   0.0000   0.0000      even
       19   0.0000   0.0000       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    Arm 1, which has no parity line, shows no detrended effect -
    so the block is the source.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.0000, arm 1 +0.0001.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

      revealed, the sign-flip still fails. Report the stratified
      test in the paper - it forecloses this objection.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.070 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played

    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.000    1.000   +1.000   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.964    1.000   +0.036   TRACKED without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        0.947    1.000   +0.053   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: USED-FIELD TRACKED. Without the block the model
    recalls the opponent's last move (0.964) but cannot
    reconstruct its own score (0.000). exp6 showed the
    last move is the ONLY field whose falsification moves
    behaviour. So the block repairs the field the model was
    already failing AND already ignoring, which is why perfect
    reading buys no behavioural change. Arm-1 CPR of ~0.00 is
    then an ARITHMETIC result and must not be reported as
    'the model cannot track the state'.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    allc  revealed       0.8525   0.8535   -0.0010   [-0.0118,+0.0101]  0.8574   1000
    tft   all turns      0.9155   0.9423   -0.0268   [-0.0355,-0.0185]  0.0001   1000
    tft   pre-reveal     0.8319   0.8611   -0.0292   [-0.0542,-0.0041]  0.0230   1000
    tft   revealed       0.9474   0.9673   -0.0198   [-0.0253,-0.0144]  0.0001   1000

    BASE RATE - episodes that EVER reach the revealed state
    opp                arm 3            arm 3b   selection gap
    allc     1,000/1,000100.0%   1,000/1,000100.0%   +0.000
    tft      1,000/1,000100.0%   1,000/1,000100.0%   +0.000

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   UNDERPOWERED   the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  REJECTED       untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    UNDERPOWERED   the objection's own test

    VERDICT: inconclusive on the revealed stratum. Only
      100.0% of episodes reach it in the smallest cell, so the
      study cannot resolve opponent-conditional play. That is a
      limitation to state, not a rejection to claim.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.000 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    3b          0.209       0.211   no true state in the block; this is a floor

       16      0.001   2,000      0.999   2,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.211. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 21.1% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.526. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

    arm  opp           n  mass p05      p50      p95  |gap| p50   <0.10   <0.25
    1    allc     20,000     0.048    0.173    0.441      0.109   0.201   0.747 *
    1    tft      20,000     0.058    0.200    0.509      0.184   0.150   0.645
    3    allc     20,000     0.077    0.230    0.608      0.167   0.093   0.559
    3    tft      20,000     0.084    0.246    0.634      0.218   0.077   0.509
    3b   allc     20,000     0.079    0.250    0.537      0.185   0.078   0.499 *
    3b   tft      20,000     0.099    0.301    0.625      0.283   0.051   0.365

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: 74.7% of decisions in the largest-effect cells sit
    below action_mass_total 0.25. That effect is partly a
    statement about a renormalisation of a small number. Report
    the fragile share beside the estimate, and check the effect
    survives restricting to solid decisions before quoting it.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0239    -0.0249     [-0.0339,-0.0157]  0.0001       +0.0358
    3b   tft       -0.0075    +0.0002     [-0.0048,+0.0051]  0.9584       +0.0978
    1    allc      +0.0110    +0.0004     [-0.0096,+0.0103]  0.9336       -0.1084
    1    tft       +0.0032    +0.0067     [+0.0012,+0.0122]  0.0158       +0.0662

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.7970   0.8560      even
        1   0.8420   0.8195       odd
        2   0.8370   0.8430      even
        3   0.8695   0.8435       odd
        4   0.8585   0.8225      even
        5   0.8815   0.8340       odd
        6   0.8875   0.8445      even
        7   0.8995   0.8235       odd
        8   0.8870   0.8410      even
        9   0.9230   0.8380       odd
       10   0.9240   0.8540      even
       11   0.9265   0.8440       odd
       12   0.9275   0.8300      even
       13   0.9190   0.8485       odd
       14   0.9005   0.8390      even
       15   0.9120   0.8380       odd
       16   0.9010   0.8180      even
       17   0.9150   0.8080       odd
       18   0.9105   0.8175      even
       19   0.8995   0.7975       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    CAUTION: arm 1 also shows a detrended parity effect in
    1|tft, and arm 1 has no parity line. Something
    other than the block produces an alternation here; do not
    attribute 3b's effect to the block until that is explained.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.0668, arm 1 -0.0211.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

    reconstructs the state from raw history 32.8% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.820. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

    'quotable' drops turn 0 only where an arm is byte-identical to

    perturbation  3b - 1    tft      +0.2043   +0.2043   [+0.1802,+0.2289]  0.0001   0 *

    * = raw bootstrap CI excludes zero, BEFORE any multiplicity
    correction. Part F decides which of these survive.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

    arm  opp           n  mass p05      p50      p95  |gap| p50   <0.10   <0.25
    1    allc     20,000     0.125    0.393    0.735      0.260   0.037   0.195
    1    tft      20,000     0.121    0.379    0.729      0.298   0.038   0.217
    3    allc     20,000     0.130    0.360    0.764      0.171   0.024   0.259 *
    3    tft      20,000     0.127    0.341    0.735      0.213   0.026   0.283
    3b   allc     20,000     0.192    0.447    0.774      0.275   0.017   0.109 *
    3b   tft      20,000     0.177    0.436    0.774      0.322   0.016   0.126

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: 25.9% of decisions in the largest-effect cells sit
    below action_mass_total 0.25. That effect is partly a
    statement about a renormalisation of a small number. Report
    the fragile share beside the estimate, and check the effect
    survives restricting to solid decisions before quoting it.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      +0.0494    +0.0837     [+0.0729,+0.0946]  0.0001       +0.4620
    3b   tft       +0.0339    +0.0708     [+0.0610,+0.0807]  0.0001       +0.5712
    1    allc      -0.0230    -0.0006     [-0.0099,+0.0089]  0.9046       +0.3222
    1    tft       -0.0194    +0.0087     [+0.0008,+0.0167]  0.0320       +0.3750

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.2420   0.2505      even
        1   0.2585   0.2595       odd
        2   0.2945   0.2800      even
        3   0.3185   0.2905       odd
        4   0.3655   0.2985      even
        5   0.4360   0.3220       odd
        6   0.7550   0.3540      even
        7   0.6535   0.3525       odd
        8   0.8235   0.3995      even
        9   0.7235   0.4320       odd
       10   0.8460   0.4720      even
       11   0.7500   0.4965       odd
       12   0.8330   0.5400      even
       13   0.7445   0.5630       odd
       14   0.8545   0.5505      even
       15   0.7705   0.5615       odd
       16   0.8390   0.6045      even
       17   0.7995   0.6285       odd
       18   0.8355   0.6360      even
       19   0.8175   0.6915       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    CAUTION: arm 1 also shows a detrended parity effect in
    1|tft, and arm 1 has no parity line. Something
    other than the block produces an alternation here; do not
    attribute 3b's effect to the block until that is explained.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.5166, arm 1 +0.3486.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.000 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

    SKIPPED - needs arm 3c and turns.donor_agent_score.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0832    -0.0641     [-0.0765,-0.0517]  0.0001       +0.3110
    3b   tft       -0.0724    -0.0566     [-0.0698,-0.0434]  0.0001       +0.2558
    1    allc      -0.0115    +0.0090     [-0.0044,+0.0228]  0.1930       +0.2604
    1    tft       -0.0196    -0.0026     [-0.0161,+0.0108]  0.7018       +0.2172

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.0915   0.1295      even
        1   0.1570   0.1815       odd
        2   0.1960   0.2400      even
        3   0.2830   0.3130       odd
        4   0.2860   0.3625      even
        5   0.4205   0.3890       odd
        6   0.3355   0.4015      even
        7   0.4455   0.4350       odd
        8   0.3950   0.4235      even
        9   0.4585   0.4465       odd
       10   0.4300   0.4560      even
       11   0.5015   0.4505       odd
       12   0.4430   0.4710      even
       13   0.5085   0.4525       odd
       14   0.4510   0.5090      even
       15   0.5140   0.4785       odd
       16   0.4420   0.4860      even
       17   0.5075   0.4810       odd
       18   0.4575   0.4840      even
       19   0.5095   0.4910       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    Arm 1, which has no parity line, shows no detrended effect -
    so the block is the source.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.2834, arm 1 +0.2388.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  D. THE 3c OVERSHOOT - three explanations, all testable
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    true-score reconstruction check: 0 at turn 0 in every episode - OK

    (i) ARE 3c's FALSIFIED ROWS LANDING ON HIGH-LEVERAGE TURNS?
        3m falsifies every turn >= 1, so its per-turn effect
        e(t) = P(D|3,t) - P(D|3m,t) IS the per-lied-row effect at
        turn t. If the move is the only operative field, 3c's
        effect should equal sum_t rate_c(t) e(t) / T. The flat
        rescale used in analysis/13 instead uses mean(rate) x
        mean(e), and the two differ by exactly the covariance
        between where 3c lies and where lying matters.

        opp      observed  reweighted  flat rescale  cov(rate,e)
        allc      -0.0140     +0.0000       -0.0000      +0.0000
        tft       -0.0337     -0.0272       -0.0266      -0.0006

        VERDICT: turn composition explains it. The reweighted
        prediction is closer to the observed 3c effect than the
        flat rescale is, so the overshoot is WHERE 3c lies, not
        a second mechanism. The open question in EXPERIMENTS.md
        can be closed with this line.

    (ii) DO THE TWO FIELDS INTERACT WHEN BOTH ARE WRONG?
        opp   cell                       P(D)     rows
        allc  move+score wrong              -        0
        allc  move only wrong               -        0
        allc  score only wrong         0.1037   19,000
        allc  neither wrong                 -        0
        allc  interaction              not estimable (a cell is below 30 rows)
        tft   move+score wrong         0.2097    4,874
        tft   move only wrong          0.1816      804
        tft   score only wrong         0.1141   13,085
        tft   neither wrong            0.1814      237
        tft   interaction             -0.0112   [-0.0425,+0.0197]  p 0.4844

        A move+score cell materially above move-only is a field
        interaction and explains the overshoot. Equal cells rule it
        out. 'Neither wrong' is the internal control: those rows are
        arm 3 wearing arm 3c's label and should match arm 3.

    (iii) POST-TREATMENT SELECTION - is 'per lied row' even a thing?
        A 3c row is falsified exactly when the donor's asserted last
        move differs from the recipient's true one. Against TFT the
        opponent's last move IS the recipient's own previous action,
        so falsification SELECTS on the recipient's own recent play.
        If it does, falsified and unfalsified rows are not
        exchangeable and effect/rate is a biased per-row estimator.

        opp   group              rows  prev turn D  any prior D  P(D) now
        allc  falsified             0            -            -         -
        allc  unfalsified      19,000       0.0978       0.4589    0.1037
        tft   falsified         5,678       0.1486       0.5687    0.2057
        tft   unfalsified      13,322       0.1264       0.4804    0.1153
        tft   SELECTION GAP                +0.0222      +0.0883

        VERDICT: SELECTED. The recipient's own prior-defection
        rate differs by up to 0.088 between falsified and
        unfalsified rows. Falsification is not as-if random within
        arm 3c, so analysis/13's 'per lied row' column is not a
        per-row causal effect and the 26-32% overshoot computed
        from it is not evidence of anything. Report the marginal
        3c effect and the falsification rate separately, and drop
        the ratio - or estimate it within (turn, history) strata
        as analysis/11 does for the score.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  E. SCORE DOSE-RESPONSE - is '29x' a dose or a field?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3s lies by a FIXED 15 points and arm 3m flips a
    binary field 100% of the time. The '29x' ratio between them is
    therefore a statement about DOSE as much as about FIELD. Arm 3c is
    the only arm whose score error varies, so it is the only arm that
    can put a slope on the score - a slope that predicts what a 15-point
    lie should do, testable against what arm 3s actually did.

    vs allc  (19,000 non-degenerate rows)
         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
             1-4   16,315      0.0966   [+0.1014,+0.1172]
             5-9    2,129      0.1301   [+0.1031,+0.1369]
           10-14      447      0.1879   [+0.0994,+0.1680]
           15-19       74      0.3108   [+0.0994,+0.2933]
             20+       35      0.3143   [+0.1500,+0.4963]

      slope, all 3c rows        +0.01025 per point   [+0.00679,+0.01368]
      slope, move NOT falsified +0.01025 per point   [+0.00676,+0.01361]   (19,000 rows)
      implied 15-point effect  +0.1538   [+0.1015,+0.2042]
      arm 3s measured effect    +0.0216   [+0.0122,+0.0310]   (P(D|3s) - P(D|3))

      VERDICT: inconsistent. The 3c dose curve does not predict
      arm 3s's measured effect, so the score response is not a
      simple magnitude response - it is a threshold, a format
      effect, or the 3c slope is confounded. Either way the
      dose defence of '29x' fails and the ratio must be
      reported as arm-specific.

    vs tft  (19,000 non-degenerate rows)
         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
               0    1,041      0.1816   [+0.1513,+0.2018]
             1-4   15,778      0.1230   [+0.1305,+0.1487]
             5-9    1,744      0.2403   [+0.1589,+0.2031]
           10-14      337      0.3175   [+0.1976,+0.3034]
           15-19       82      0.4756   [+0.2147,+0.4446]
             20+       18                below min bin size

      slope, all 3c rows        +0.01897 per point   [+0.01496,+0.02283]
      slope, move NOT falsified +0.01745 per point   [+0.01306,+0.02197]   (13,322 rows)
      implied 15-point effect  +0.2618   [+0.1959,+0.3295]
      arm 3s measured effect    +0.0303   [+0.0177,+0.0427]   (P(D|3s) - P(D|3))

      VERDICT: inconsistent. The 3c dose curve does not predict
      arm 3s's measured effect, so the score response is not a
      simple magnitude response - it is a threshold, a format
      effect, or the 3c slope is confounded. Either way the
      dose defence of '29x' fails and the ratio must be
      reported as arm-specific.

    The 3c slope inherits analysis/11's confound: |error| is large
    exactly when the recipient's own score ran unusually, which happens
    when it was already defecting. Read the slope as an upper bound.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  F(inputs). CONTRAST FAMILY  (episode-level, 10,000 resamples)
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    'quotable' drops turn 0 only where an arm is byte-identical to
    arm 3 there (3c, 3m). Arm 3s is
    falsified at turn 0 too, so dropping it would discard real
    manipulation - analysis/13 drops it from every contrast.

    contrast                opp      incl t0  quotable               95% CI       p  t0
    ATE_true       3 - 3b   allc     -0.0118   -0.0118   [-0.0227,-0.0009]  0.0350   0 *
    ATE_true       3 - 3b   tft      -0.0193   -0.0193   [-0.0324,-0.0060]  0.0038   0 *
    content_move   3 - 3m   allc     -0.0880   -0.0931   [-0.1043,-0.0820]  0.0001   1 *
    content_move   3 - 3m   tft      -0.0846   -0.0891   [-0.1023,-0.0756]  0.0001   1 *
    content_score  3 - 3s   allc     -0.0216   -0.0216   [-0.0311,-0.0119]  0.0002   0 *
    content_score  3 - 3s   tft      -0.0303   -0.0303   [-0.0426,-0.0181]  0.0001   0 *
    content_donor  3 - 3c   allc     -0.0131   -0.0140   [-0.0233,-0.0045]  0.0032   1 *
    content_donor  3 - 3c   tft      -0.0324   -0.0337   [-0.0462,-0.0211]  0.0001   1 *
    perturbation  3b - 1    allc     -0.2192   -0.2192   [-0.2363,-0.2017]  0.0001   0 *
    perturbation  3b - 1    tft      -0.2083   -0.2083   [-0.2261,-0.1908]  0.0001   0 *

    * = raw bootstrap CI excludes zero, BEFORE any multiplicity
    correction. Part F decides which of these survive.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  G. NEAR-TIE / ACTION MASS - how solid is each P(defect)?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    P(defect) is the Cooperate/Defect masses renormalised to sum to 1.
    Below action_mass_total 0.1 the run calls it off-task. Between
    0.1 and 0.25 it is neither off-task nor solid:
    the renormalisation is dividing by a small number.

    arm  opp           n  mass p05      p50      p95  |gap| p50   <0.10   <0.25
    1    allc     20,000     0.998    0.999    0.999      0.596   0.000   0.000 *
    1    tft      20,000     0.998    0.999    0.999      0.554   0.000   0.000
    3    allc     20,000     1.000    1.000    1.000      0.865   0.000   0.000
    3    tft      20,000     1.000    1.000    1.000      0.865   0.000   0.000
    3b   allc     20,000     0.999    0.999    1.000      0.865   0.000   0.000 *
    3b   tft      20,000     0.999    0.999    1.000      0.864   0.000   0.000
    3c   allc     20,000     1.000    1.000    1.000      0.830   0.000   0.000
    3c   tft      20,000     0.999    1.000    1.000      0.809   0.000   0.000
    3m   allc     20,000     0.999    1.000    1.000      0.704   0.000   0.000
    3m   tft      20,000     0.999    1.000    1.000      0.704   0.000   0.000
    3s   allc     20,000     1.000    1.000    1.000      0.809   0.000   0.000
    3s   tft      20,000     0.999    1.000    1.000      0.809   0.000   0.000

    * = a cell in this database's largest contrast.
    VERDICT: only 0.0% of decisions in the largest-effect
    cells are fragile (mass < 0.25). The estimates are not
    an artefact of near-ties.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  H. 3b ROUND PARITY - is the placebo leaking state?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3b's block contains 'Round parity: even/odd'. That is one bit
    of the turn index, and under a known 20-round horizon the turn
    index is weakly endgame-relevant. If 3b defection tracks parity, or
    accelerates near the horizon in a way arm 1 does not, the placebo
    carries usable state and ATE_true is biased toward zero.

    'even-odd' is the RAW contrast and is confounded by the turn
    trend. 'detrended' differences each odd turn against its two
    neighbours, so a locally linear trend of ANY slope cancels. Read
    the detrended column; the raw one is shown only for continuity.

    arm  opp      even-odd  detrended                95% CI       p  last5-first5
    3b   allc      -0.0314    -0.0256     [-0.0324,-0.0186]  0.0001       +0.0742
    3b   tft       -0.0308    -0.0256     [-0.0341,-0.0173]  0.0001       +0.1082
    1    allc      -0.0278    -0.0067     [-0.0176,+0.0043]  0.2138       +0.2468
    1    tft       -0.0338    -0.0128     [-0.0239,-0.0014]  0.0268       +0.2992

    P(defect) BY TURN INDEX  (arm 3b; arm 1 control)
     turn       3b     arm1    parity
        0   0.0200   0.0205      even
        1   0.0655   0.1775       odd
        2   0.0480   0.1805      even
        3   0.0885   0.1945       odd
        4   0.0750   0.2420      even
        5   0.1190   0.2920       odd
        6   0.0975   0.2990      even
        7   0.1205   0.3190       odd
        8   0.0865   0.3160      even
        9   0.1270   0.3490       odd
       10   0.1035   0.3575      even
       11   0.1155   0.3860       odd
       12   0.1060   0.3735      even
       13   0.1570   0.3875       odd
       14   0.1235   0.4065      even
       15   0.1640   0.4115       odd
       16   0.1405   0.4275      even
       17   0.1370   0.4155       odd
       18   0.1470   0.4635      even
       19   0.1645   0.4620       odd

    VERDICT: THE PLACEBO RESPONDS TO PARITY in 3b|allc, 3b|tft.
    The effect survives local detrending, so it is not the turn
    trend. Arm 3b is therefore NOT contentless: it carries one bit
    of the turn index and the model acts on it.
    CAUTION: arm 1 also shows a detrended parity effect in
    1|tft, and arm 1 has no parity line. Something
    other than the block produces an alternation here; do not
    attribute 3b's effect to the block until that is explained.

    CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PAPER: ATE_true = P(D|3) - P(D|3b) compares
    the treatment against a control that is itself partly active.
    Report the detrended parity coefficient in Methods, state that
    ATE_true is conservative, and do not describe 3b as
    'non-diagnostic' without this qualification. The pre-registered
    null is not overturned by it - a leaky placebo biases toward
    finding nothing, which is the direction already reported.

    Endgame slope: 3b +0.0912, arm 1 +0.2730.
    Raw slopes are NOT comparable here: arm 3b's defection level is
    a fraction of arm 1's (the container effect), so its absolute
    slope is mechanically smaller. A smaller endgame slope under 3b
    is the container effect compressing the trajectory, not evidence
    that the parity line is doing endgame work. Compare on the odds
    scale before drawing any conclusion from this row.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  A. REVEALED-OPPONENT STRATIFICATION
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Until the agent defects, TFT and ALLC have emitted an identical
    sequence, so no arm contains a bit that distinguishes them. The
    pre-registered sign-flip is untestable on those turns.

    opp   stratum        P(D|3)  P(D|3b)       ATE              95% CI       p   n_ep
    allc  all turns      0.6974   0.6369   +0.0604   [+0.0471,+0.0737]  0.0001   1000
    allc  pre-reveal     0.6115   0.5724   +0.0391   [+0.0105,+0.0678]  0.0100   1000
    allc  revealed       0.7280   0.6721   +0.0559   [+0.0432,+0.0686]  0.0001   1000
    tft   all turns      0.6875   0.6748   +0.0128   [+0.0003,+0.0252]  0.0446   1000
    tft   pre-reveal     0.6136   0.5841   +0.0296   [+0.0012,+0.0580]  0.0432   1000
    tft   revealed       0.7182   0.7131   +0.0052   [-0.0069,+0.0170]  0.4132    999

    BASE RATE - episodes that EVER reach the revealed state
    opp                arm 3            arm 3b   selection gap
    allc     1,000/1,000100.0%   1,000/1,000100.0%   +0.000
    tft      1,000/1,000100.0%     999/1,000 99.9%   +0.001

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   REJECTED       the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  REJECTED       untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    UNDERPOWERED   the objection's own test

    VERDICT: inconclusive on the revealed stratum. Only
      100.0% of episodes reach it in the smallest cell, so the
      study cannot resolve opponent-conditional play. That is a
      limitation to state, not a rejection to claim.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.001 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model llama   readout scratchpad

    arm          allc         tft   what a CPR of 1.000 would mean here
    1           0.200       0.200   reconstruction from raw history - the real denominator
    3           1.000       1.000   every probe target printed verbatim; certifies COPYING
    3b          0.200       0.200   no true state in the block; this is a floor
    3m          0.340       0.535   scored against the truth while shown a lie; 0.000 expected

    ARM 1 CPR BY TURN INDEX  (pooled over opponents; arm 3 for scale)
     turn   arm1 CPR       n   arm3 CPR       n
        0      1.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
        4      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
        8      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
       12      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
       16      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.200. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 20.0% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -0.500. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  I. WHICH FIELD FAILS - decomposing arm-1 CPR
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model llama   readout scratchpad

    ARM 1 - state must be reconstructed from [HISTORY]
     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played
                     ARITHMETIC                RECALL              COUNTING
        0                 1.000                 1.000                 1.000
        4                 0.000                 0.867                 1.000
        8                 0.000                 0.932                 1.000
       12                 0.000                 0.923                 1.000
       16                 0.000                 0.925                 1.000

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.000    1.000   +1.000   FAILS without the block
    opponent's last move  RECALL          0.912    1.000   +0.088   TRACKED without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        1.000    1.000   +0.000   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: USED-FIELD TRACKED. Without the block the model
    recalls the opponent's last move (0.912) but cannot
    reconstruct its own score (0.000). exp6 showed the
    last move is the ONLY field whose falsification moves
    behaviour. So the block repairs the field the model was
    already failing AND already ignoring, which is why perfect
    reading buys no behavioural change. Arm-1 CPR of ~0.00 is
    then an ARITHMETIC result and must not be reported as
    'the model cannot track the state'.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

    SKIPPED - arm 3c absent from this database.

    SIGN-FLIP (predicted: TFT negative, ALLC positive)
      all turns   UNDERPOWERED   the pooled test, as pre-registered
      pre-reveal  UNDERPOWERED   untestable by construction - a verdict
                                 here is noise, printed only to show
                                 the pooled number is a blend of two
                                 strata that answer different questions
      revealed    UNDERPOWERED   the objection's own test

    VERDICT: inconclusive on the revealed stratum. Only
      0.1% of episodes reach it in the smallest cell, so the
      study cannot resolve opponent-conditional play. That is a
      limitation to state, not a rejection to claim.

    CAUTION, PRINTED EVERY TIME: 'has defected at least once' is an
    OUTCOME. Conditioning on it breaks randomisation between arms 3
    and 3b, so the revealed-stratum ATE is a descriptive contrast in a
    selected population. The selection differs between arms by up to
    0.008 here; that gap is the size of the bias, and it must be
    quoted beside the estimate. This is not a causal effect.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  C. ARM 1 CPR - the denominator the title question needs
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model mistral   readout logit

    arm          allc         tft   what a CPR of 1.000 would mean here
    1           0.400       0.400   reconstruction from raw history - the real denominator
    3           1.000       1.000   every probe target printed verbatim; certifies COPYING
    3b          0.400       0.400   no true state in the block; this is a floor
    3c          0.600       0.200   belief in the donor's numbers, not comprehension
    3m          0.804       0.830   scored against the truth while shown a lie; 0.000 expected
    3s          0.000       0.000   scored against the truth while shown a lie; 0.000 expected

    ARM 1 CPR BY TURN INDEX  (pooled over opponents; arm 3 for scale)
     turn   arm1 CPR       n   arm3 CPR       n
        0      1.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
        4      1.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
        8      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
       12      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000
       16      0.000   2,000      1.000   2,000

    VERDICT: arm-1 CPR = 0.400. Without a block, the model
    reconstructs the state from raw history 40.0% of the time.
    THAT is the denominator of 'does comprehension cause defection?'.
    Arm 3's CPR is not a comprehension measure at all: the block prints
    every probe target verbatim, so 1.000 there is consistent with pure
    copying and cannot license a claim about understanding.
    Turn drift (turns 15+ minus turns 0-4): -1.000. Falls with turn index -> a working-memory result.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  I. WHICH FIELD FAILS - decomposing arm-1 CPR
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    model mistral   readout logit

    ARM 1 - state must be reconstructed from [HISTORY]
     turn             own score  opponent's last move         rounds played
                     ARITHMETIC                RECALL              COUNTING
        0                 1.000                 1.000                 1.000
        4                 1.000                 1.000                 1.000
        8                 0.000                 1.000                 1.000
       12                 0.000                 1.000                 1.000
       16                 0.000                 1.000                 1.000

    NON-TRIVIAL PROBES ONLY (turn 0 excluded - at turn 0 every field
    is 0/None, so a correct answer there is not a tracking result)
    field                 task            arm 1    arm 3   repair   reading
    own score             ARITHMETIC      0.250    1.000   +0.750   partial
    opponent's last move  RECALL          1.000    1.000   +0.000   TRACKED without the block
    rounds played         COUNTING        1.000    1.000   +0.000   TRACKED without the block

    VERDICT: MIXED. Report per field and do not quote the
    all-three CPR as a single denominator.

    Whatever the verdict, report CPR per field. A conjunction over
    three tasks of unequal difficulty is governed by the hardest one,
    and exp6 proved the three fields are not behaviourally equivalent.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  D. THE 3c OVERSHOOT - three explanations, all testable
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    true-score reconstruction check: 0 at turn 0 in every episode - OK

    (i) ARE 3c's FALSIFIED ROWS LANDING ON HIGH-LEVERAGE TURNS?
        3m falsifies every turn >= 1, so its per-turn effect
        e(t) = P(D|3,t) - P(D|3m,t) IS the per-lied-row effect at
        turn t. If the move is the only operative field, 3c's
        effect should equal sum_t rate_c(t) e(t) / T. The flat
        rescale used in analysis/13 instead uses mean(rate) x
        mean(e), and the two differ by exactly the covariance
        between where 3c lies and where lying matters.

        opp      observed  reweighted  flat rescale  cov(rate,e)
        allc      +0.0000     +0.0000       -0.0000      +0.0000
        tft       -0.0003     -0.0011       -0.0020      +0.0009

        VERDICT: turn composition explains it. The reweighted
        prediction is closer to the observed 3c effect than the
        flat rescale is, so the overshoot is WHERE 3c lies, not
        a second mechanism. The open question in EXPERIMENTS.md
        can be closed with this line.

    (ii) DO THE TWO FIELDS INTERACT WHEN BOTH ARE WRONG?
        opp   cell                       P(D)     rows
        allc  move+score wrong              -        0
        allc  move only wrong               -        0
        allc  score only wrong         0.0000    8,000
        allc  neither wrong                 -        0
        allc  interaction              not estimable (a cell is below 30 rows)
        tft   move+score wrong         0.0015    2,644
        tft   move only wrong               -        0
        tft   score only wrong         0.0001   14,356
        tft   neither wrong                 -        0
        tft   interaction              not estimable (a cell is below 30 rows)

        A move+score cell materially above move-only is a field
        interaction and explains the overshoot. Equal cells rule it
        out. 'Neither wrong' is the internal control: those rows are
        arm 3 wearing arm 3c's label and should match arm 3.

    (iii) POST-TREATMENT SELECTION - is 'per lied row' even a thing?
        A 3c row is falsified exactly when the donor's asserted last
        move differs from the recipient's true one. Against TFT the
        opponent's last move IS the recipient's own previous action,
        so falsification SELECTS on the recipient's own recent play.
        If it does, falsified and unfalsified rows are not
        exchangeable and effect/rate is a biased per-row estimator.

        opp   group              rows  prev turn D  any prior D  P(D) now
        allc  falsified             0            -            -         -
        allc  unfalsified       8,000       0.0001       0.0010    0.0000
        tft   falsified         2,644       0.0000       0.0026    0.0015
        tft   unfalsified      14,356       0.0005       0.0052    0.0001
        tft   SELECTION GAP                -0.0005      -0.0026

        VERDICT: balanced. Prior-defection differs by only
        0.003 between falsified and unfalsified rows, so the
        rescale is approximately unbiased and the overshoot needs
        one of the other two explanations.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  E. SCORE DOSE-RESPONSE - is '29x' a dose or a field?
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    Arm 3s lies by a FIXED 15 points and arm 3m flips a
    binary field 100% of the time. The '29x' ratio between them is
    therefore a statement about DOSE as much as about FIELD. Arm 3c is
    the only arm whose score error varies, so it is the only arm that
    can put a slope on the score - a slope that predicts what a 15-point
    lie should do, testable against what arm 3s actually did.

    vs allc  (8,000 non-degenerate rows)
         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
             1-4    8,000      0.0000   [+0.0000,+0.0000]

      slope, all 3c rows        +nan per point   [+nan,+nan]
      slope, move NOT falsified +nan per point   [+nan,+nan]   (8,000 rows)
      implied 15-point effect  +nan   [+nan,+nan]
      arm 3s measured effect    +0.0003   [+0.0001,+0.0006]   (P(D|3s) - P(D|3))

      VERDICT: inconsistent. The 3c dose curve does not predict
      arm 3s's measured effect, so the score response is not a
      simple magnitude response - it is a threshold, a format
      effect, or the 3c slope is confounded. Either way the
      dose defence of '29x' fails and the ratio must be
      reported as arm-specific.

    vs tft  (17,000 non-degenerate rows)
         |error|     rows   P(defect)                95% CI
             1-4   17,000      0.0004   [+0.0001,+0.0006]

      slope, all 3c rows        -0.00042 per point   [-0.00077,-0.00014]
      slope, move NOT falsified -0.00017 per point   [-0.00043,+0.00000]   (14,356 rows)
      implied 15-point effect  -0.0026   [-0.0064,+0.0000]
      arm 3s measured effect    -0.0001   [-0.0003,+0.0002]   (P(D|3s) - P(D|3))

      VERDICT: consistent. A 15-point lie moves defection by about
      what the 3c dose curve predicts, so the score field IS
      described by a magnitude response and the move-vs-score
      ratio is partly a statement about dose. Say 'a 15-point
      score error', never 'the score field', when quoting 29x.

    The 3c slope inherits analysis/11's confound: |error| is large
    exactly when the recipient's own score ran unusually, which happens
    when it was already defecting. Read the slope as an upper bound.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  F(inputs). CONTRAST FAMILY  (episode-level, 10,000 resamples)
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    'quotable' drops turn 0 only where an arm is byte-identical to
    arm 3 there (3c, 3m). Arm 3s is
    falsified at turn 0 too, so dropping it would discard real
    manipulation - analysis/13 drops it from every contrast.

    contrast                opp      incl t0  quotable               95% CI       p  t0
    ATE_true       3 - 3b   allc     -0.0003   -0.0003   [-0.0005,+0.0000]  0.0654   0  
    ATE_true       3 - 3b   tft      -0.0005   -0.0005   [-0.0010,-0.0001]  0.0210   0 *
    content_move   3 - 3m   allc     -0.0110   -0.0115   [-0.0132,-0.0100]  0.0001   1 *
    content_move   3 - 3m   tft      -0.0133   -0.0141   [-0.0163,-0.0119]  0.0001   1 *
    content_score  3 - 3s   allc     -0.0003   -0.0003   [-0.0007,-0.0001]  0.0176   0 *
    content_score  3 - 3s   tft      +0.0001   +0.0001   [-0.0002,+0.0003]  0.8198   0  
    content_donor  3 - 3c   allc     +0.0000   +0.0000   [-0.0002,+0.0002]  1.0000   1  
    content_donor  3 - 3c   tft      -0.0003   -0.0003   [-0.0006,+0.0001]  0.1614   1
